C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

D3820th of 30

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. Wide in the accounts and narrow in the value. Six units, Belron in 42 countries, TVH in 170, PHE in seven, Automotive in one, all sharing nothing operationally, and the perimeter is widening at the edges (Spain, Ireland, TVH bolt-ons). That width lowers group-level predictability (one-line group guidance, equity accounting, minorities, four EPS bases), but because one asset carries about three-quarters of value and 57 percent of operating profit, the economically relevant perimeter is narrow.

Modelling implication. Model Belron in detail (volume, penetration, price and mix, margin, finance costs, currency), PHE and Automotive at moderate detail, TVH and Moleskine simply, and the holding layer (corporate costs, holding interest, tax, share count, minorities) explicitly. Belron's revenue is 55 percent North America, 32 percent eurozone, 13 percent other (mostly sterling and Canadian dollar).

Group judgement

Risks

C+608th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts for the next 18 months are more predictable than the structure of the company suggests and less predictable than the record of its main asset alone would imply. Three independent bodies of work converge on two operating drivers (Belron's operating result and an Automotive floor) and two risks (insurer pricing on Belron's realised price; the correlated US-consumer, rate and currency stress), and the corrections this set makes to the earlier ones (segment arithmetic, the share of growth from interest relief, the company's record of raising guidance, the mechanics of PHE's puts and Belron's dividends) all adjust levels within the same picture rather than the picture itself. The expected change rests about nine-tenths on strengths the business has shown; the exception that matters is Automotive's cost-out, which the business has not done before and which Belgian process will slow. The drivers are concentrated in one asset, one customer type and one capital structure, but the concentrated driver is the one with the best disclosure and the tightest agreement, and the diffuse remainder is small. The cone is narrow for FY2026 (EPS EUR 13.45 to 13.85), moderate for FY2027 (EUR 14.4 to 16.2, about 65 percent probability within 5 percent of the base) and skewed down for FY2028 and beyond, where the 2029 refinancing and the pricing regime sit. Disclosure is adequate at the operating level, thin below it and twice-yearly, so the next four months must be monitored through Boyd, the US insurers, NAGS, Febiac and the Belgian unions. The share price moves on its own news (81 percent idiosyncratic), so the multiple will answer to the 10 March 2027 results and to the Belron process rather than to the sector. For the memo: present the base case as a two-driver forecast with a left tail; treat FY2026 as known within 1.5 percent; weight the 10 March 2027 event as the point at which the forecast's predictability is tested and the narrative (I-03) can change.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

B747th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The expected change, on the second set's base case rather than on the company's ambitions, rests about nine-tenths on demonstrated strengths. The two drivers that carry the FY2026 to FY2028 growth (Belron's operating result, about EUR 153m of the EUR 274m rise in PBT GS, and the Automotive floor, about EUR 38m) are of different kinds: the first is the continuation of a delivery record that is among the longest and cleanest in European services (margin from 5.4 percent in 2017 to 23.0 percent in FY2025, 24.4 percent in H2 2025 and plus 160 basis points in H1 2026; organic growth 5.9, 6.9, 7.3 percent across the last three readings; 89 percent cash conversion; leverage 5.4x to 4.3x in eighteen months while paying EUR 400m of dividends); the second is a cost-out in a shrinking market that the business has not had to perform before, under a Belgian consultation process that takes six to eighteen months. The company's own FY2028 ambitions for Automotive (about 4 percent), TVH (16 percent) and Moleskine (15 percent) do require strengths not yet shown, but the base case already discounts them (3.0, 14.5 and about 7 percent), so the memo's forecasts do not depend on them. The remaining undemonstrated element inside the base case is small and named: PHE's Spanish procurement synergy (about EUR 8m to 10m of PBT GS by FY2029) and the belief that US insurers will claw back calibration and glass pricing gradually rather than abruptly, which is a judgement about a customer's behaviour, not about a strength of the company's.

Driver by driver: demonstrated, partly demonstrated, or to be acquired.

  • Belron margin to above 25 percent by FY2028 (demonstrated). Bernstein's decomposition of the 17-point margin gain since 2018 (7 points operating leverage, 6 points calibration and complexity, the rest cost transformation) shows two engines that still run: workshop density (a two-technician site is loss-making below two replacements a day and reaches a 40 percent margin at eight) and content per job (ADAS brackets on 60 percent of windscreens in 2025 from 49 percent in 2020; penetration 52.1 percent with "above 60 percent" targeted). H2 2025 at 24.4 percent means the FY2028 target is within 60 basis points already; Bernstein models 26.0 percent. The strength the thesis needs here is the one Belron has: converting content and density into margin while the buyer pushes back. What it does not need is a new strength; what it needs is that an external force (insurer pricing) stays gradual.
  • Belron volume (demonstrated, cyclical). Volume returned (+3.9 percent in H1 2026, North America +9.4 percent) as premium inflation faded (US motor premiums minus 5.1 percent year on year in August 2026); glass-only claim frequency is 1.5 percent against a five-year average of 2.1 percent, so the recovery has room. The State Farm administration mandate (from July 2025) is a demonstrated, contractual volume source. The strength is Belron's; the risk is the US consumer.
  • Belron finance relief (demonstrated, largely done). The repricings (July 2025, January 2026), the October 2025 step-down and the USD Term Loan B reduction from 4,655m to 4,036m are facts; the further EUR 30m a year at 100 percent in the model needs only continued cash generation.
  • Automotive to 2.6 percent in FY2027 and 3.0 percent in FY2028 (partly demonstrated). The business has run at about 3 percent in normal pre-pandemic years and held 3.1 percent through a 19 percent delivery fall in 2020, so a 3 percent margin on a stable market is within its history. What it has not done is cut a fixed retail cost base by 10 to 15 percent while losing 150 basis points of share a year to Chinese entrants and absorbing Volkswagen's tighter distribution terms. The precedents (Bilia's overhead cuts; Inchcape's GBP 90m of 2020 savings, over half permanent) say such programmes stick in part; the Belgian precedents (Audi Brussels six months of consultation, Caterpillar Gosselies two years) say the timing slips. Bernstein's 1.7 percent FY2028 margin is the bear case for this driver. I keep 3.0 percent but regard it as the least demonstrated part of the base case, worth about EUR 57m of PBT GS (EUR 0.80 of EPS) between the base and the bear.
  • PHE (demonstrated, with one new element). Organic growth of 3.9 to 6.0 percent against a market of about 2 percent, margins held at 9.1 to 9.6 percent through deflation and re-inflation, EUR 1.1bn of acquired revenue integrated: the roll-up competence is proven. Halving the Spanish post-synergy multiple through procurement is new in scale (Polaris and Regueira about EUR 340m of sales, Suministros Dama 80 percent in March 2026), and a former LKQ executive's point that parts "sitting in Germany do not mean anything to France" limits cross-border synergy to procurement, which is where PHE says it lies. Small in PBT GS.
  • TVH to 14.5 percent in FY2028 (demonstrated in 2024 at 15.6 percent). H1 2026 organic growth of 6.6 percent and PBT GS plus 16.9 percent, with the ramp-up costs in Poland and Reno still inside the margin (13.7 percent), make 14.5 percent a recovery, not an acquisition of capability; a new chief executive (Giuliano Parody, from 1 September 2026) removes the leadership gap the first set flagged.
  • Moleskine (not demonstrated; immaterial). Organic growth of 6.2 percent in H1 2026 and 11.3 percent in Q2 is a turn from two years of decline; the 15 percent ambition is not in the base case.
  • The holding's capital discipline (demonstrated). The EUR 500m bridge prepaid in June 2025, corporate net debt from EUR 653m (end-2024) to EUR 287m (end-2025), EUR 218m of subsidiary dividends received in H1 2026 against EUR 106m paid out: the centre funds itself.

The strength the thesis does not have and does not need inside 18 months. A listed Belron, a Belron dividend policy, a chief executive with a record at D'Ieteren. Each would matter for the multiple and for FY2029 onward; none is in the FY2026 to FY2027 earnings.

Where the plausibility is weakest, in order. (1) Automotive's restructuring timing and the 2027 company-car tax cliff, because the business has no precedent of its own for a cost-led margin rebuild. (2) The assumption that insurer pricing bites gradually through rolling two-to-three-year renewals rather than through a coordinated flat-rate move; the evidence (State Farm, GEICO, Allstate flat rates of USD 390 to 550; Travelers studying hourly rates; Liberty Mutual within 14 months) is of gradual, carrier-by-carrier action, which supports the base case. (3) The persistence of the US volume recovery into a weakening consumer.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?D4620th of 30full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

B586th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The cone is narrow for FY2026, moderate for FY2027 and moderately wide and skewed down for FY2028; over the 18 months that matter for the memo (to the FY2027 results in March 2028) the case is more predictable than its holding structure, leverage and sector make it look, because about two-thirds of PBT GS comes from a business whose demand is non-discretionary, whose pricing is contracted and indexed, and whose half-year results have landed within a few percent of expectations for four years. On the second set's base case I put FY2026 EPS at EUR 13.45 to 13.85 (base EUR 13.63; the second half is a swing of about plus or minus EUR 15m of PBT GS), FY2027 at EUR 14.4 to 16.2 (base EUR 15.60; stress minus 7.5 percent, bull plus 4 percent) and FY2028 at EUR 15.9 to 18.6 (base EUR 17.68; stress minus 10 percent, bull plus 5 percent). The probability that FY2027 PBT GS lands within 5 percent of EUR 1,127m is about 65 percent and within 10 percent about 90 percent. The skew is downward and grows with the horizon, for three reasons: the base case already sits near the top of what the operating record supports for Belron's margin, the risks are correlated (a weak US consumer, insurer cost pressure and higher US rates arrive together), and two of the diffuse drivers (Automotive, the 2029 refinancing) have fatter lower tails than upper tails. Against sector peers, the cone is narrower than car retail's (Inchcape cut its margin guidance 50 basis points in one quarter; LKQ withdrew guidance) and wider than a pure parts distributor's, because of leverage and Automotive.

What this set adds to the second set's cone, item by item.

  • Belron margin (upside to FY2026 to FY2027, not previously counted). H2 2025 at 24.4 percent and H1 2026 at 23.0 percent (plus 160 basis points) make the Analyst's FY2026 margin of 23.9 percent conservative by 20 to 30 basis points, worth about EUR 7m to 10m of PBT GS; Bernstein's FY2028 path reaches 26.0 percent against the Analyst's 25.3 percent, a EUR 28m difference at group share. I treat this as a modest upward skew in Belron's operating line that offsets the downward items below in the 18-month window.
  • Belron pricing (downside, FY2027 onward, already partly in the base case). The fourth-set evidence firms up the mechanism: flat-rate calibration caps of USD 390 to 550 at State Farm, Allstate and GEICO; Travelers' hourly-rate study (about USD 200 against flat fees of USD 250 to 400); Liberty Mutual within 14 months; State Farm's ADAS Map documentation requirement; contracts renewed on two-to-three-year cycles. The second set's base case carries about a third of a 20 percent US calibration price cut by FY2028 (EUR 33m of PBT GS for the whole); the stress case carries all of it. The new fact that calibration is only 8 percent of Belron's revenue caps the direct effect; the larger and less visible exposure is the mark-up over NAGS on complex glass, which no insurer has yet moved on publicly.
  • Belron volume and weather (two-sided, short-horizon). The 2012 precedent (a mild winter, organic sales minus 4.4 percent, operating result minus 24 percent) is the cleanest example of Belron's symmetric leverage: a 3-point volume shortfall in the winter half of 2027 would cost about EUR 45m to 50m at 100 percent (EUR 24m at group share, EUR 0.33 of EPS, about 2 percent of FY2027) and would land in the half that sets the tone for the 10 March 2027 guidance. The 2020 precedent (volumes minus 13 percent, EBITDA margin held above 10 percent) shows the labour cost can be flexed when the shock is visible. US glass-only claim frequency at 1.5 percent against a 2.1 percent five-year average leaves room for volume to recover further; US motor premiums falling 5.1 percent year on year (August 2026) ease claims avoidance but also sharpen insurers' cost focus.
  • Belron finance costs (downside, mostly beyond 18 months). With 77 percent of debt floating and the hedged share undisclosed, a December 2026 Fed increase and higher-for-longer SOFR add perhaps EUR 5m to 15m at 100 percent to FY2027 against the Analyst's EUR 385m; the 2029 refinancing at 7.5 percent adds about EUR 41m a year at 100 percent from Q4 2029 (the Analyst assumed about half). These widen FY2029 to FY2030 by about EUR 10m to 20m of PBT GS on the downside; they barely touch FY2027.
  • Automotive (downside, FY2027). The Renault Act consultation began on 3 September 2026; Belgian precedents ran six months (Audi Brussels) to two years (Caterpillar Gosselies), and the company has disclosed neither savings nor costs. The second set's EUR 15m of net savings in FY2027 assumes a social plan by mid-2027; a slip to late 2027 costs about EUR 8m. The 2027 end of full deductibility for corporate electric vehicles (Jefferies, 15 October 2026) is a second-leg risk to fleet volumes that the base case carries as revenue minus 2.9 percent; a 10 percent fleet drop would be about minus EUR 10m to 15m more. Bernstein's 1.7 percent FY2028 margin is the bear tail (about minus EUR 57m against the base). Automotive net financial debt rose to EUR 409m at June 2026 (from EUR 260m), so its below-operating costs are drifting up, a few million a year.
  • PHE puts (cash, not EPS). The EUR 194m put at end-2026 is priced at an EBITDA multiple; the strike may be EUR 200m to 215m. The anticipated-acquisition method means no EPS effect; the Analyst's FY2027 acquisitions line of minus EUR 244m may be about EUR 15m light. The EUR 307m of subsidiary-level puts (49 percent of Polaris and Regueira) have undisclosed exercise terms; if exercisable early, a second cash call could fall inside the window. This is a liquidity and net-debt item, funded, and it does not move the earnings cone.
  • Currency and tax (two-sided). Plus or minus 5 percent in EUR/USD is plus or minus EUR 0.27 of EPS; the adjusted tax rate is implied at 28 percent and not guided; a point is 1.4 percent of EPS.

The stress and bull cases restated (PBT GS, my estimate).

  • FY2027 stress (about minus EUR 85m, minus 7.5 percent): Belron margin 60 basis points below base and volume 1 point below on a weak US consumer and a mild winter; US floating rates plus 100 basis points on the unhedged half; EUR/USD 1.25; Automotive at 2.2 percent with savings slipping to late 2027; TVH flat. These are correlated except the weather, so the joint probability is perhaps 15 percent.
  • FY2027 bull (about plus EUR 45m, plus 4 percent): Belron margin at 25.0 percent a year early with volume holding at 3 percent plus; Automotive at 3.0 percent on an early social plan; PHE Spanish synergies in the first full year; a ratings-driven margin step-down at Belron. Probability perhaps 20 percent.
  • FY2028 stress (about minus 10 percent): the full US calibration price cut, Bernstein's Automotive margin, rates plus 100 basis points, EUR/USD 1.25, tax 29 percent. FY2028 bull (about plus 5 percent): Belron at 26 percent, Automotive at 3.5 percent, a Belron ratings upgrade.

Why the skew is down even though the Belron operating lean is up. The upside items are incremental and bounded (a few tens of basis points of margin on a business already near its target); the downside items are discrete and can compound (a pricing regime change by one large insurer, a social-plan delay, a rate move on EUR 5.5bn of floating debt, a winter). The base case is a good central estimate; the distribution around it has a longer left tail.

Comparison with how the sector behaves. Over FY2025 to FY2026, D'Ieteren's group result landed plus 3.1 percent (FY2025) and minus 0.3 percent (H1 2026) against company-compiled consensus while its Automotive segment missed by 51 percent: the portfolio absorbed a single-segment collapse that would have sunk a pure distributor. That is the empirical case for a moderate cone, and it is the reason the memo can treat 18-month predictability as a relative strength of this case despite the holding structure.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

B567th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. Moderately helpful, with one large hole in timing and several in content. The company publishes exactly what is needed to track the thesis (Belron's organic growth split into volume and price and mix, calibration penetration, adjusted operating margin, free cash flow and leverage; segment PBT GS for all six units; Automotive deliveries, share and order book; PHE and TVH organic growth), but it publishes it twice a year, so in the next six months there is one company event that matters, the FY2026 results and FY2027 guidance on 10 March 2027, and nothing before it except the chief executive handover in December, weekly buyback notices and whatever the Belron process produces. Between now and March the Consumer must monitor the thesis through third parties, which for this company is unusually feasible because the two drivers that matter have good external proxies: US glass claims and insurer behaviour are visible through Boyd Group, Driven Brands and the listed insurers, and Belgian registrations are published monthly by Febiac. The items the company does not disclose at all, and which the fourth set could not find elsewhere, are the ones the memo will most want: the hedged share of Belron's floating debt, the size and timing of Automotive's savings, Belron's dividend policy, the exercise terms of the subsidiary puts, calibration's share of revenue and margin (Bernstein's 8 percent is an estimate), and anything quarterly.

What the company gives, and when.

  • 10 March 2027 (FY2026 results). Expect: PBT GS against the "low-to-mid-single-digit growth at constant currency" guidance; Belron FY2026 organic growth with the volume and price-and-mix split, penetration (about 54 to 55 percent expected), margin (about 24 percent), leverage (about 3.9 to 4.1x), free cash flow and dividends paid; Automotive FY2026 deliveries, share (about 21.5 percent), order book (about 25,000 at June) and the first quantified word on the transformation plan if the social plan is agreed; PHE's first full statement on Spain; TVH's second half; FY2027 guidance (the first under Eric Machiels); the FY2026 dividend (consensus EUR 2.10) and the next buyback. The constant-currency framing means a reported miss can coexist with guidance met; read the constant-currency figure.
  • Late May 2027 (Q1 2027 trading update, as on 28 May 2026). Belron's Q1 organic growth, which in 2026 was the only quarterly operating datum the group published; at the edge of the six-month window.
  • Nothing else scheduled. No Q3 update; Belron's quarterly lender reporting is private. A listing prospectus, if the process reaches that stage, would add a Belron-level disclosure stream (quarterly results, a dividend policy, the hedge ratio), which would be the single largest improvement in monitorability the case could receive.

What third parties give, and why it is good enough for six months.

  • US volume and pricing (Belron, about 55 percent of revenue). Boyd Group's Q3 2026 results (November) and Q4 (March 2027) for repairable claims volume (flat to minus 2 percent in Q2 2026) and calibration internalisation; Driven Brands' Auto Glass Now same-store sales (2.6 percent in Q2 2026, from 11.2 percent); Progressive's monthly results and Allstate's and Travelers' Q4 2026 reports (January to February 2027) for physical-damage severity and any public calibration policy; the NAGS quarterly list-price update (the September 2025 update was plus 5.6 percent); US winter weather in December to February (the 2012 precedent); expert and trade-press reports on State Farm's ADAS Map enforcement and Travelers' hourly-rate study.
  • Automotive. Febiac monthly registrations by brand (the Belgian market, Volkswagen Group's share, the BEV share, which was 32.6 percent in August 2026 with D'Ieteren holding 28.8 percent of Belgian BEVs in 2025); Belgian union and trade-press reports on the Renault Act consultation (the company and the unions have been silent since September); Volkswagen Group's Q3 results (30 October 2026) and any change to Belgian distribution terms; the 1 January 2027 company-car tax changes and fleet order commentary.
  • PHE and TVH. Inter Cars' monthly sales and MEKO's and LKQ's quarterly reports for European parts pricing (LKQ's German ERP failure is a share opportunity for PHE in the Benelux); KION's orders and Komatsu's Komtrax data for TVH's end markets; the Cary Group sale process (CVC and Nordic Capital exploring an exit at above EUR 3bn) for a read on European glass valuations.
  • Balance sheet and credit. Belron's bond prices and any rating action (Fitch's upgrade trigger is leverage well below 4.0x with a post-dividend free cash flow margin above 2 percent); the Fed's December decision for the floating debt; D'Ieteren's weekly buyback filings (337,711 shares by 15 September 2026 under the EUR 100m programme).

Where disclosure is unhelpful or misleading, and how to handle it. (1) The group guides one line at constant currency; build the constant-currency bridge yourself (EUR/USD 1.17 assumed). (2) FactSet's consolidated lines exclude Belron and TVH; never read the group from them. (3) The company's net financial debt definition excludes EUR 501m of put options; add them. (4) Adjusted results exclude about EUR 130m a year of recurring "adjusting items" at Belron; read reported earnings and cash alongside. (5) Belron's and TVH's PBT GS lines are at 50.3 and 40 percent; PHE's is at 100 percent under the anticipated-acquisition method despite 91 percent legal ownership. (6) Segment finance costs are not disclosed directly; derive them from operating result less PBT at 100 percent.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

D4619th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The trace is clear from the company's statements to the operating lines, blurred between the operating lines and PBT GS, and absent from PBT GS to EPS, which the analyst must build. Each FY2028 ambition names a measurable operating variable (Belron above 25 percent margin and above 5 percent organic growth; TVH 16 percent; Automotive about 4 percent; Moleskine 15 percent; corporate adjusted EBIT of EUR 10m to 15m; above EUR 5bn of cumulative trading cash flow 2025 to 2028), and the company reports the drivers behind the one that matters (penetration, the volume and price-and-mix split, margin). What it does not say is how finance costs, tax and the share count carry those lines to EPS, and those three items decide about a third of the EPS path. The external transmission is clear where the value chain is disclosed (ADAS fitment and penetration to price and mix to margin; Belgian registrations to Automotive revenue to a 24 percent decremental margin; parts prices to PHE revenue with pass-through) and opaque where it runs through confidential contracts (insurer flat rates and NAGS discounts to Belron's realised price). The memo can present a complete transmission map; it must label the two links it has to infer.

The internal trace, line by line.

  • Belron: ambition to operating result (clear). Organic growth = volume (claims frequency times share; frequency 1.5 percent against 2.1 percent average; the State Farm mandate) plus price and mix (NAGS-indexed glass price on about 84 percent of revenue, calibration fees of about EUR 150 on 8 percent, value-added products 2 percent). Margin = density (loss-making below two jobs a day per site, 40 percent at eight) plus content (calibration at 80 to 90 percent incremental gross margin) plus the Transformation Program (whose system-integrator fees of EUR 44m in 2025 sit outside adjusted results). Sensitivities: 1 point of organic growth is about EUR 0.16 to 0.20 of EPS; 100 basis points of margin is about EUR 40m of PBT GS, EUR 0.55 of EPS (3 percent).
  • Belron: operating result to PBT GS (blurred). Finance costs of about EUR 416m at 100 percent in FY2026 (derived) depend on Term Loan B margins (known: SOFR plus 200, Euribor plus 225), reference rates (known), the hedged share (unknown), lease interest (partly known) and deleveraging (about EUR 30m a year at 100 percent). Then times 50.3 percent. Deutsche Bank's "over EUR 140m by FY2028" saving and the second set's EUR 160m are both inferences.
  • Automotive: ambition to PBT (clear in mechanics, opaque in the one number that matters). Revenue = Febiac market (about 425,000 in 2026 heading to 400,000) times share (21.5 percent) times price and mix (negative); margin = a personnel-heavy retail cost base with a 24 percent decremental, to be cut by 344 roles (about EUR 30m to 35m gross a year on my arithmetic; the company has given no figure); below the operating line about EUR 20m to 25m of interest on net debt of EUR 409m (June 2026). 50 basis points of margin is EUR 22m of PBT GS, EUR 0.30 of EPS.
  • PHE (clear, with the ownership link now resolved). Organic growth (mid-single-digit) plus acquisitions at about 9.1 to 9.6 percent margin, 100 percent attributed under the anticipated-acquisition method, less PHE's finance costs on EUR 1.39bn of debt (Euribor plus 250 on the add-on), less, possibly, the 49 percent of Polaris and Regueira (the second set deducts a few million; the method may attribute 100 percent of those too).
  • TVH (clear). Organic growth and margin at 100 percent, less finance costs (EUR 12.1m in H1 2026), times 40 percent.
  • Corporate, Immo, Moleskine (now clearer). Corporate and Unallocated PBT GS minus 23.0 in FY2025 and minus 8.5 in H1 2026 (bridge-loan interest gone); Immo's EUR 28.6m of net rental income at a 7.0 percent yield sits inside it, 80 percent of it from Automotive; Moleskine minus 8.0 in FY2025, minus 4.7 in H1 2026; the FY2028 corporate target of plus EUR 10m to 15m of EBIT.
  • PBT GS to EPS (absent; built). Tax at about 28 percent (implied from FY2025, not guided; the reported consolidated rate of 43 percent is meaningless); average ordinary shares about 52.4m falling about 1 percent a year through the buyback (weekly disclosure); the 5m participating shares outside the denominator; the brokers' basis EPS = PBT GS times 0.72 divided by shares. The company never shows this calculation; the fact that the consensus mean is pulled down by two brokers on other bases (EUR 9.81 and 10.00 for FY2026) shows how many analysts fail to make it.

The external transmission, value chain to EPS.

  • Vehicle technology to Belron price and mix (clear, disclosed). ADAS brackets on 60 percent of windscreens (2025) from 49 percent (2020), rain sensors 74 percent, acoustic glass 70 percent; penetration 52.1 percent; glass-only claim severity USD 750 in Q1 2024 against a USD 495 five-year average. This chain is in the company's and Bernstein's numbers.
  • Insurer behaviour to Belron realised price (opaque, inferred). The flat-rate caps, the ADAS Map requirement, the hourly-rate studies and the NAGS discount formulas (a large insurer paying about 50 percent of list) are known from expert calls, not from the company; the share of Belron's North American insurance volume affected and the timing of renewals are confidential. This is the one link the memo must flag as inferred; it is also the link with the most consequence for FY2028.
  • US premium cycle to Belron volume (clear with a lag). Premium inflation of about 40 percent (2022 to 2024) produced claims avoidance and flat volume; deflation (minus 5.1 percent in August 2026) is producing recovery; the frequency gap to the average measures the headroom.
  • Glass input cost to Belron margin (clear). Tariffs and supply raised glass costs 10 to 20 percent; NAGS updates quarterly, so pass-through lags about three months; domestic sourcing (Fuyao, Ohio) protects Safelite relative to importers.
  • Belgian policy to Automotive (clear). Company-car deductibility, subsidy withdrawal, mobility budgets from 2027, the EU hybrid import quota (350,000 units a year) and Volkswagen's paused agency model all trace to registrations and mix; the mechanics are visible monthly.
  • European parts prices and the industrial cycle to PHE and TVH (clear, small). Pass-through at PHE; KION's flat orders and Komatsu's data for TVH.
  • Rates to EPS (half clear). Reference rates are public; the hedge is not; the band is EUR 15m to 30m at group share per 100 basis points.

What the memo should present. A one-page transmission map with the two inferred links (insurer pricing to realised price; the hedge ratio) marked, and the three analyst-built items (finance costs, tax, shares) shown with their sensitivities. The company's communication is disciplined at the operating level and reticent below it; the reticence is structural (equity accounting, constant-currency guidance, one group line), not evasive, and a listed Belron would remove most of it.

C-81What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?C+489th of 30full answer under 2a Internal risks
C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

B355th of 30

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. Material, and higher than usual for this stock now, because the share price has come to depend on one perceived event (the Belron listing) in a market regime that punishes complexity, leverage and car retail. The largest are a failed, delayed or under-priced Belron listing, a sector or regime sell-off that drags the "car-retail half" of the shares, and a public insurer move on calibration that reframes Belron's growth. Low liquidity and a standing seller amplify each.

Reasoning and evidence.

  • Listing: if priced below the EUR 30 to 40bn range or pulled for market conditions, the "Belron tracker" is marked down; the review closing without a transaction would remove the main catalyst.
  • Read-across: Inchcape fell 9.3 percent on 22 October 2026 on a margin cut; a failed large European IPO; Boyd or Driven Brands warnings; Volkswagen headlines; a US insurer's public calibration policy.
  • Regime: risk-off, Momentum Underweight, Fragility Double Underweight; holding discounts widen in risk-off; the regime note's reversal trigger is a US 10-year below 4.5 percent.
  • Technicals: average daily volume about 54,700 shares (three months), about USD 20 to 25m a day; free float limited by 49.54 percent family ownership; SPDG selling about 10 percent over five years; Belgian retail selling after the 2024 withholding; negligible short interest (no short-covering support).
  • New-CEO risk: a first strategy statement led by acquisitions.

Track record (guidance raise/sell-side upgrades) ~L2-3Y

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?C+749th of 30full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

B627th of 30

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement. The company does not upgrade during the year; it sets a low bar, confirms it through shocks and beats it. This is the "sandbagging precision" archetype at group level: dependable for avoiding misses, uninformative about the upside. Mid-term ambition has been raised once in the period (Belron from 23 percent to above 25 percent by FY2028 after hitting 23 percent), which is the closest thing to an upgrade. The behaviour is the institution's more than the outgoing chief executive's, and the CFO who runs it stays, but a new chief executive tends to reset the bar on arrival.

Reasoning and evidence. Beats against guidance in FY2022 to FY2025; FY2023 delivered EUR 970.8m against original guidance; FY2026 guidance held through a 66.6 percent fall in Automotive's PBT; sell-side description "a proven conservative sandbagger"; Belron's FY2025 margin target met; TVH guided a declining FY2026 operating result and then delivered +17 percent PBT in H1 2026 (a de facto beat, not an upgrade).

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

B+723rd of 30

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. At group level, a consistent record of beating guidance and broadly beating or meeting consensus over one, three and five years, including in the difficult FY2025 (currency, interest, US claims avoidance). The misses are at segment level and outside Belron. The pattern the Consumer should carry into the model: the group headline is protected by portfolio diversification and conservative guidance; the segment that surprises positively is Belron; the segments that surprise negatively are those with operational or cyclical problems (Automotive, TVH, Moleskine).

Reasoning and evidence.

  • One year: FY2025 adjusted PBT GS EUR 955.6m against company-compiled consensus EUR 926.6m (+3.1 percent), helped by Automotive; FY2026 guidance initially 7 to 9 percent below consensus (a negative surprise in March 2026); H1 2026 EUR 482.4m against EUR 483.6m (minus 0.3 percent): Belron +5 percent, TVH +20 percent, PHE +5 percent, Automotive minus 51 percent (EUR 36.4m against EUR 74.4m).
  • Three and five years: beats against guidance and consensus in FY2022, FY2023, FY2024 and FY2025 (change-forces research).
  • Segment misses: TVH missed its 2025 targets by more than 12 percent on sales and more than 30 percent on free cash flow; Moleskine repeated declines; Jefferies cut its FY2025 PBT estimate 4 percent to EUR 887m during the year on Automotive and TVH, after which the group beat.
  • Comparable conditions: FY2025's environment (strong euro, high rates, consumer pressure) resembles the next 12 months; the group still beat.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

B+603rd of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The forecasts deserve high weight for FY2026, moderate weight for FY2027 and FY2028, and low weight for FY2029 to FY2030, and the lean differs by segment more than by year. The group headline record (beats against guidance in FY2022 to FY2025; H1 2026 within 0.3 percent of consensus) supports leaning FY2026 slightly up. The segment record says lean Belron and the finance line up, Automotive, TVH and Moleskine down, and PHE slightly up; because Belron's operating upside in my model is already tempered (incremental margins below recent levels), the net lean for FY2027 is neutral and for FY2028 slightly down. The new chief executive (December 2026) changes the presentation risk (a cautious FY2027 framing on 10 March 2027 is likely) more than the delivery risk, because the CFO who runs the budgeting and Belron's management both stay.

The record, segment by segment, and what it implies.

  • Group. Beats against guidance and consensus in FY2022 to FY2025; FY2025 PBT GS EUR 955.6m against company-compiled consensus EUR 926.6m (+3.1 percent); FY2026 guidance set 7 to 9 percent below consensus in March 2026 and then confirmed through a 66.6 percent fall in Automotive's PBT. Implication: the group sets bars it can clear; the guidance is a floor, not a forecast. Lean FY2026 up by 1 to 2 percent against consensus.
  • Belron. H1 2026 +5 percent against consensus, margin 23.0 percent against 21.8 percent expected; FY2025 margin target (23 percent) met at 22.9 to 23.0 percent; financing charges lower than consensus "had fully modelled". Implication: lean Belron's operating result and finance line up; I have taken only part of that lean (my FY2027 Belron PBT GS of EUR 732m is about 15 percent above FY2026), because the pricing balancing loop is new and has no track record yet.
  • Automotive. H1 2026 adjusted PBT EUR 36.4m against EUR 74.4m expected (minus 51 percent); management was "less transparent" about share loss; FY2028 ambition of about 4 percent is above the pre-pandemic level of about 3 percent. Implication: lean down; my FY2027 margin of 2.6 percent assumes the restructuring delivers on time, and a further miss of 50 basis points is the likeliest single negative surprise in the 18-month window.
  • TVH. Missed 2025 Capital Markets Day targets by more than 12 percent on sales and more than 30 percent on free cash flow; then beat H1 2026 by 20 percent; Jefferies cut TVH's operating forecasts by more than 20 percent in September 2026 on end-market softness. Implication: lean down against the 16 percent target (I use 14.5 percent for FY2028); the volatility is large but TVH is only about 7 percent of PBT GS.
  • PHE. H1 2026 +5 percent against consensus; organic growth 3.9 to 6.0 percent against a market of about 2 percent; margin held through deflation. Implication: lean slightly up; Spain is the uncertainty.
  • Moleskine. Operating profit down 34 percent (2024) and 50 percent (2025); EUR 76.4m loan waiver; a modelled EUR 140m write-down (ING). Implication: lean down; immaterial to EPS (about 1 percent).

The cone of outcomes on my base case (EPS, brokers' basis).

  • FY2026: EUR 13.45 to 13.85 (base EUR 13.63). H1 is reported; the H2 swing is about plus or minus EUR 15m of PBT GS (Automotive's H2 and Belron's H2 margin).
  • FY2027: EUR 14.60 to 16.20 (base EUR 15.60). Stress: Belron margin 60 basis points lower, Automotive at 2.2 percent, rates +100 basis points on the unhedged term loans, EUR/USD 1.25: about minus EUR 74m of PBT GS (minus 6.5 percent). Bull: Belron margin 40 basis points higher, Automotive at 3.0 percent, PHE synergies early: about +EUR 42m (+3.8 percent).
  • FY2028: EUR 16.00 to 18.60 (base EUR 17.68). Stress: Belron margin 100 basis points lower (the full US calibration price cut), rates +100 basis points, EUR/USD 1.25, Automotive at 2.5 percent, tax 29 percent: about minus 9 percent. Bull: Belron margin 70 basis points higher, Automotive at 3.5 percent, a ratings upgrade, PHE synergies: about +5 percent. The stress elements are correlated (a weak US consumer, insurer cost pressure and higher rates arrive together), so they belong in one case.
  • FY2029 to FY2030: plus or minus 12 to 15 percent. Dominated by Belron's terminal margin, the 2029 refinancing and, at the edge of the horizon, self-calibration.

Weights to give the forecasts. FY2026: high (about 85 percent of the year's PBT GS is either reported or follows from known rates and contracts). FY2027: moderate (the outcome rests on two named drivers, Belron's operating growth and the Automotive floor). FY2028: moderate to low (it adds the pricing loop). FY2029 to FY2030: low; use them for direction and for the shape of the deceleration, not for valuation precision.

Disagreement with the first set. The first set's management judgement says management credibility supports "leaning slightly above the FY2026 guidance" and taking Belron's targets at face value through FY2028. I agree for FY2026 and for Belron's margin target (my path meets it); I would not take the Automotive (4 percent), TVH (16 percent) and Moleskine (15 percent) targets at face value, and together these account for most of the gap between my FY2028 and the company's implied ambition.

Financial forecasts

D5415th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts are good for an investment case in the near term and adequate further out, and their quality is better than the first set and the prior memo described, but their concentration is higher. On my build, FY2026 PBT GS grows about 4 percent reported (about 5.5 percent at constant currency, the top of guidance) because Belron's finance relief of about EUR 50m at group share absorbs most of Automotive's collapse; FY2027 and FY2028 grow about 13.6 and 12.3 percent, which is consensus, but the growth comes from operations (Belron's operating result, an Automotive floor, PHE with Spain), not from finance relief, which falls to about EUR 15m a year. That correction matters in two directions: it makes the FY2027 to FY2028 growth more deserving of a multiple (it is not financial engineering), and it makes it more exposed to one operating variable, Belron's price and mix, at the moment US insurers start to regulate it. Beyond FY2028 growth slows to 7 to 9 percent as both Belron engines fade, which is below the two-broker FY2029 consensus. The holding's cash lags its earnings: free cash flow to D'Ieteren is about 40 percent of adjusted net income in FY2026 to FY2027 and rises toward 57 percent only as Belron's dividends grow from FY2028. For the Consumer, the steer is: treat FY2026 as near-certain at EUR 13.4 to 13.9 of EPS; treat FY2027 as a two-driver forecast (Belron's operating growth of about 9 percent and Automotive's restructuring) with a cone of about minus 6.5 to plus 4 percent; treat FY2028 as consensus-like with a downside skew (minus 9 to plus 5 percent) from correlated US-insurer, US-consumer, rate and currency risks; and test whether March 2027 guidance under a new chief executive frames FY2027 below a consensus that the business can still deliver.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

B646th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Below the operating line the next four years reverse the last two: costs below the group-share operating result rose from about EUR 220m (FY2023) and EUR 263m (FY2024) to EUR 412m (FY2025) after the 2024 recapitalisation, absorbing 30 percent of the operating result; they fall to about EUR 344m (FY2026) and then drift down to about EUR 310m (FY2030), back to about 17 to 18 percent of the operating result, close to the pre-recapitalisation level. Tax (about 28 percent adjusted), minorities and the share count (minus about 1 percent a year) are stable contributors. The items that matter most for the Consumer are not in adjusted EPS at all: recurring "adjusting items" (system-integrator fees, restructuring, warehouse closures) of about EUR 130m a year at Belron alone, Automotive restructuring cash of EUR 40m to 60m in FY2027, and EUR 194m of PHE minority puts maturing at the end of 2026. They make adjusted EPS about 5 to 7 percent higher than an economic EPS, and make free cash flow to D'Ieteren only about 40 percent of adjusted net income in FY2026 to FY2027, rising to about 57 percent by FY2030 as Belron's dividends grow.

Item by item.

  • Interest and other finance costs (group share, derived and my estimate). Belron at group share EUR 260m (FY2025), EUR 209m (FY2026), EUR 194m, EUR 179m, EUR 169m and EUR 158m. PHE finance costs rise from about EUR 70m (FY2025) to about EUR 79m (FY2026) and EUR 86m (FY2027) on the Spanish debt, then ease. Automotive below its operating result about minus EUR 22m a year (H1 2026 minus EUR 10.9m against minus EUR 5.5m in H1 2025, after the EUR 400m dividend to Corporate raised its net debt to EUR 259.5m). Corporate interest falls after the bridge repayment (Corporate's adjusted pre-tax loss narrowed to minus EUR 8.5m in H1 2026 from minus EUR 16.7m) but rises again slightly if D'Ieteren funds the EUR 194m PHE put.
  • Tax. The adjusted rate is implied, not guided: 28.2 percent in FY2025 (EUR 12.95 times about 53.1m shares over EUR 955.6m). The reported consolidated rate (43.3 percent FY2025, 55.4 percent FY2024) is distorted by equity accounting and non-deductible items (share-based payments, Moleskine impairments) and must not be used. I hold 28 percent; a move to 29 to 30 percent (higher Belgian or US rates, interest-limitation rules biting less as debt falls is a small offset) costs about 1.4 to 2.8 percent of EPS per point. The EUR 72.6m tax-dispute liability reversed in 2025 shows that disputes can move reported figures without touching adjusted ones.
  • Minorities. Belron's 49.7 percent and TVH's 60 percent outside ownership are netted in group share. Inside PHE: the 9 percent at the holding company (puts of EUR 194.0m maturing end-2026) and 49 percent of Polaris and Regueira (puts within EUR 307.4m). Spain's minorities take about EUR 2.5m (FY2026) rising to about EUR 11m (FY2029) of PBT. I keep PHE's group-share fraction unchanged in my PBT GS path because the files do not say whether the 9 percent is already counted under the anticipated-acquisition method; if exercise raises group share to 100 percent, PBT GS rises about EUR 20m (EUR 0.28 of EPS) from FY2027, less about EUR 8m of holding interest. I include the EUR 194m as an FY2027 cash outflow in the forecast inputs.
  • Adjusting items excluded from adjusted EPS. Belron's other adjusting items EUR 131.9m in 2025 (system-integrator fees EUR 44.3m, warehouse closures EUR 31.0m, restructuring EUR 19.3m); system-integrator fees have appeared every year (EUR 57.1m, 25.1m and 44.3m in 2023 to 2025), so about EUR 40m a year at 100 percent (EUR 20m GS, EUR 0.28 of EPS) is a recurring economic cost. Purchase-price amortisation in 2025: Belron EUR 31.4m, TVH EUR 44.5m, PHE EUR 25.9m at 100 percent (about EUR 57m at group share). Expected one-offs: Automotive restructuring (FY2026 to FY2027), the Moleskine write-down, possibly further Automotive goodwill in March 2027. Reported basic EPS (EUR 8.04 in FY2025) will therefore stay 35 to 40 percent below adjusted EPS; the gap is not closing.
  • Share count. About 53.69m ordinary shares and 5m participating shares (the latter receive one-eighth of the ordinary dividend and are not in the brokers' EPS denominator); treasury shares 1,443,038 (2.7 percent) on 21 October 2026. The EUR 100m programme (December 2025) had bought 337,711 shares (about EUR 58m) by 15 September 2026: about EUR 80m a year. I assume about EUR 80m (FY2026), EUR 90m (FY2027) and EUR 100m a year after, average shares 52.4m, 52.0m, 51.55m, 51.1m and 50.65m: about +1 percent a year to EPS. SPDG's sale of its remaining 10.6 percent over five years supplies the float; half of any buyback outperformance goes to the family's foundations, a small leakage. The earlier programme was suspended in 2024 for the recapitalisation, so the buyback is discretionary.
  • Net income. Adjusted net income GS EUR 688m (FY2025) to EUR 714m, EUR 811m, EUR 912m, EUR 987m and EUR 1,052m. Against history: FY2022 to FY2025 grew about 8 percent a year through a recapitalisation that added about EUR 180m a year of interest; the next three years grow about 10 percent a year, the two after about 7 percent.
  • Free cash flow to D'Ieteren (my definition: consolidated EBITDA less cash tax, interest, leases, capital spending, working capital and cash adjusting items, plus dividends from Belron and TVH; before acquisitions, buybacks and dividends). About EUR 292m (FY2026), EUR 316m (FY2027), EUR 428m (FY2028), EUR 521m (FY2029), EUR 597m (FY2030), against the company's own free cash flow of EUR 507.0m (FY2024) and EUR 373.9m (FY2025). The fall in FY2026 is Automotive's lost EBITDA (about EUR 140m), Spain's working capital and restructuring cash; the rise from FY2028 is Belron's dividend. Free cash flow per share about EUR 5.6 (FY2026) to EUR 11.8 (FY2030), against adjusted EPS of EUR 13.6 to 20.8. A look-through measure (group share of Belron's own free cash flow instead of its dividend) would be about EUR 250m to EUR 350m a year higher, but that cash is not D'Ieteren's to spend.
  • Uses of cash against it. Dividends (EUR 2.00 for FY2025 rising to about EUR 3.20 by FY2030: about EUR 106m to EUR 165m a year), the buyback, acquisitions (EUR 176m in FY2026 including Spain; EUR 244m in FY2027 including the PHE put; about EUR 60m a year after). Holding-level cash is roughly balanced to FY2027 and in surplus of about EUR 140m to EUR 280m a year from FY2028, which is the capacity for larger buybacks that a new chief executive could choose, or redirect to acquisitions.

Versus the last four to six years. The FY2020 to FY2025 period was dominated by perimeter changes (TVH 2021, PHE 2022), the 2024 EUR 74 per share special dividend and the recapitalisation. The next four years are the first since 2021 with a stable perimeter, so the below-the-line items become predictable: interest falling slowly, tax flat, share count shrinking slowly. The two discontinuities to watch are the 2029 Belron note refinancing and any Belron recapitalisation after a listing.

P-10

Pulling together all the above answers, in all sets so far, what is your carefully considered base case for the key operational drivers and the key financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

D5315th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The base case is a Belron-led compounding path on a shrunken but stabilising Automotive base: PBT GS EUR 992m (FY2026), EUR 1,127m (FY2027), EUR 1,266m (FY2028), EUR 1,371m (FY2029) and EUR 1,461m (FY2030); EPS EUR 13.63, 15.60, 17.68, 19.32 and 20.77 (+5.3, +14.5, +13.3, +9.3 and +7.5 percent). It is mechanically sound and evidenced, but concentrated, which is what the Consumer should test in D-03 and D-04.

Margins/growth vs. past

C-53How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?B626th of 30full answer under 4c Financial profile
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

C+688th of 30

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. Meaningful scope from a few big areas, mostly congruent with strategy: Belron's margin from 23 to above 25 percent (about EUR 150m of operating profit at 100 percent on about EUR 7.5bn of revenue), Belron's finance costs falling with leverage (about EUR 100m-plus at 100 percent by FY2028), Automotive back toward 3 to 4 percent, TVH toward 15 to 16 percent, PHE's Spanish synergies, and Moleskine to break-even. Belron's targets look achievable on its own precedent; the Automotive and Moleskine targets look stretched against their own histories.

Reasoning and evidence.

  • Belron: 160 basis points in a single half against the 200-plus needed over three years; the precedent is Belron's own North American business; no external peer runs a comparable margin.
  • Automotive: the 4 percent target compares with about 3 percent pre-pandemic; FY2024 to FY2025's 4.7 to 5.1 percent was flattered by the order backlog and battery-electric mix; I would model 2.5 to 3.5 percent by FY2028 (the prior memo used 2.8 percent).
  • TVH: 15.6 percent in FY2024 shows 15 to 16 percent is achievable in a normal cycle.
  • Moleskine: 15 percent against 6.5 percent in FY2025 and two years of decline: least credible.
  • Scale of the whole: on the prior memo's arithmetic, the group-share operating result rises from EUR 1,367m to about EUR 1,580m by FY2028 and adjusted PBT GS grows about 9.5 percent a year as finance costs fall.
C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

C+689th of 30

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. The core growth engine has accelerated, not decelerated; the reported group revenue line is below its normal rate only because of currency and the shrinking Automotive arm. The one sign of deceleration inside the engine is the price and mix leg at Belron (5.1 to 3.4 percent), which is partly arithmetic as volume returns and partly the first visible effect of insurer resistance; it is the variable to watch.

Reasoning and evidence.

  • Engine accelerating: Belron organic 5.9 percent (FY2025) to 7.3 percent (H1 2026); volume flat to +3.9 percent; calibration penetration +6.2 points year on year; North America +9.4 percent.
  • Reported line below normal: group-share sales minus 0.4 percent in H1 2026 against about +2 percent a year over FY2023 to FY2025; consolidated revenue minus 3.0 percent in H1 2026; Automotive minus 10.8 percent; currency minus 2.2 percent at the operating line.
  • Input force: camera fitment and safety mandates rising; claims avoidance easing. The forward risk is a tougher H2 2026 comparison (Belron's H2 2025 operating profit +20 percent) and a weakening US consumer.
C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

B+554th of 30

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Consensus forecasts a step-up from flat to mid-teens EPS growth (FY2026 +3.5 percent, FY2027 +16.2 percent, FY2028 +15.2 percent) on revenue growth of only minus 1 to +4 percent, so the gap to the recent past is entirely margin, finance-cost relief and the end of the Automotive decline. On the change forces and foundations above, the gap is credible in direction and roughly right in size, internally consistent with segment arithmetic, but more fragile in composition than the headline suggests: it needs Belron to keep compounding margin while its debt cost keeps falling, and needs Automotive to stop falling in FY2027.

Line by line (FactSet consensus median, 2026-10-28).

  • Revenue (consolidated: Automotive, PHE, Moleskine): EUR 7,952m, 8,298m, 8,525m (minus 1.0, +4.3, +2.7 percent) against FY2023 to FY2025 of EUR 7,984m, 8,155m, 8,034m (flat organically after the PHE consolidation). Segment consensus: Automotive EUR 4,423m (minus 11 percent), 4,324m, 4,408m; PHE EUR 3,336m (+14 percent with Spain), 3,666m, 3,886m; TVH (100 percent) EUR 1,759m, 1,842m, 1,934m; Belron EUR 6,256m, 6,682m, 7,126m, of which FY2026 is implausible against H1 2026 (revenue up about 4.5 percent reported) and should be replaced.
  • EBITDA (brokers' basis, consolidated): EUR 627m, 674m, 714m against FY2025's EUR 681m; margin 7.9, 8.1, 8.4 percent against 8.5 percent: Automotive's fall, then recovery.
  • EBIT (consolidated): EUR 406m, 456m, 460m (5.1 to 5.5 percent) against EUR 392m (4.9 percent).
  • EPS (brokers' adjusted): EUR 13.40, 15.56, 17.93 (FY2029 EUR 21.55, two brokers) against EUR 12.95; implied adjusted PBT GS about EUR 980m, 1,120m, 1,280m.
  • Dividend: EUR 2.10, 2.40, 2.73 against EUR 2.00.
  • Free cash flow (consolidated): EUR 354m, 424m, 443m against FY2025's EUR 436m; capital expenditure EUR 118m to 126m.
  • Equity: EUR 2,222m rising to EUR 3,481m by FY2028 (retained earnings including equity-accounted Belron).

Credibility. FY2026: credible, slight upside (C-68). FY2027: requires about EUR 140m more adjusted PBT GS; my bridge finds about EUR 135 to 175m (Belron about EUR 90 to 100m from operating growth of 8 to 10 percent and lower finance costs; PHE's full Spanish year about EUR 20 to 25m; TVH about EUR 10m; Automotive EUR 5 to 35m from restructuring savings and a floor; Moleskine and corporate a few million), so credible if currency is stable and there is no refinancing shock. FY2028: requires Belron near 25 percent margin and continued deleveraging; credible on Belron's record, with about 40 percent of the growth from finance-cost relief (prior memo).

P-04After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?C4412th of 30full answer under 5a Pricing power
P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

D5215th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The net impact is a step from a flat-earnings, falling-quality period (FY2024 to FY2026) to a period of about 10 percent a year EPS growth (FY2026 to FY2028) that decelerates to about 7 to 8 percent (FY2029 to FY2030), led by Belron's operating result rather than by finance costs, on revenue growth that stays low (group share about 3.4 percent a year). Against the last four to six years, revenue growth is higher than FY2023 to FY2025 (about 1.8 percent a year) but far lower than the acquisition-driven FY2021 to FY2023; operating margin expansion is about half the recent pace; finance costs move from the largest drag to a small tailwind; and free cash flow to the holding rises, but only from FY2028. The pattern for the Consumer to test is "good FY2027, adequate FY2028, slowing after": the growth is front-loaded into the two years in which Belron's margin still rises by about 80 basis points a year and Automotive recovers from its trough.

The main lines, history against forecast.

The net effect by source (PBT GS, FY2025 to FY2028, +EUR 310m). Belron operations +EUR 223m (72 percent), Belron finance relief +EUR 81m (26 percent), PHE +EUR 66m (21 percent), TVH +EUR 17m (5 percent), Corporate, Immo and Moleskine +EUR 24m (8 percent), Automotive minus EUR 101m (minus 33 percent). From FY2026 to FY2028 (+EUR 274m): Belron operations +EUR 153m, Belron finance relief +EUR 31m, PHE +EUR 42m, Automotive +EUR 38m, TVH +EUR 14m, other minus EUR 3m.

What is different from the last four to six years, and why it matters.

  • Concentration rises. Belron's share of PBT GS goes from 54 percent (FY2025) to 64 percent (FY2026) and 65 percent (FY2028 to FY2030). The group becomes more of a Belron tracker in earnings, not only in value, which raises the importance of the one balancing loop (insurer pricing) and lowers the importance of Automotive after FY2026.
  • Growth quality improves. FY2026's growth is almost entirely finance relief; FY2027 to FY2028's is mostly operations. A market that discounts finance-driven growth (the first set's pattern 5) should value FY2027 to FY2028 growth more highly than FY2026's.
  • Revenue stays low growth. Group-share revenue grows about 3.4 percent a year (FY2025 to FY2030), below the company's "mid-single-digit" FY2028 group ambition, because Automotive shrinks from 41 percent of group-share revenue (FY2025) to 32 percent (FY2030) and grows about zero.
  • Cash catches up late. Free cash flow to the holding falls in FY2026 and recovers only from FY2028 as Belron's dividends rise; the cash yield on the current market capitalisation (about EUR 9.0bn on 52.25m ordinary shares outstanding at EUR 171.3) rises from about 3.3 percent (FY2026) to about 6.7 percent (FY2030).

Disagreement with the first set. The first set's closing steer said finance costs fall "about EUR 45 to 50m a year at group share on the H1 2026 run-rate while leverage falls toward 3x". That rate is right for FY2026 against FY2025, but it is not a run-rate: from the H1 2026 level, the annual group-share saving is about EUR 15m. Using EUR 45 to 50m a year for FY2027 and FY2028 would overstate FY2028 PBT GS by about EUR 60m (EUR 0.85 of EPS, about 5 percent).

Forecasts and upgrades

Probably upgrades this financial year to

B526th of 30

Memo topic 7, Scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months: the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

A572nd of 30

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Guidance, the sell-side and the narrative reflect our assessment unevenly, and the unevenness is the opportunity and the risk in equal measure. Guidance is conservative by institution and will be framed conservatively again on 10 March 2027 by a new chief executive, so the guidance events of the next 18 months are a source of beats (FY2026 at the top of its range; FY2027 delivered above its framing; a better-than-even chance of a mid-year raise in September 2027 on the pattern of 2022 and 2023) with one scheduled disappointment in the framing itself. Consensus for FY2026 to FY2028 is within 2 percent of our base case, has stopped falling, and will dip in March and recover through 2027 as the Automotive cuts prove complete and Belron's operating growth is recognised as operating; the three-year consensus (FY2029, two brokers) is about 10 percent too high and will be cut as coverage broadens. The narrative knows the asset and the event thoroughly, knows the pricing risk only as an expert-call debate, does not know the Automotive savings number or the put cash, and attributes the discount's closing to a listing that brings no cash to D'Ieteren and that the better precedents say must be paired with buybacks. Put together: for the next 18 months the gap between our assessment and what is already reflected is slightly favourable in guidance (beats to come), neutral in estimates (right level, improving slope after a dip) and unfavourable in narrative (the bull case is already told, the bear case is not); the share price reflects none of the three, which is the memo's central tension and belongs with the fair multiple and the return (topics 10 to 12). The steer to the Consumer: date the thesis's momentum turn to the second quarter of 2027 (after the March framing, before the September raise test), name the two unarticulated negatives as the things that could prevent it, and present the Belron listing as a price-discovery event whose effect on the discount depends on what the new chief executive does with Belron's cash.

Guidance

3 answers
C-1340C-6850I-0156
C-13To what extent has financial guidance been raised, or have mid-term targets or capital-markets-day ambitions been set or raised?D4321st of 30full answer under 2b Internal corporate change forces
C-68To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?B+575th of 30full answer under 6a Guidance conservatism
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

B+604th of 30

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement. The sequence I expect is: FY2026 guidance met at or above the top of its range on 10 March 2027 (about 85 percent probability of met-or-beaten, about 55 percent of a result above the "mid" end); FY2027 guidance set that day below the growth the business can deliver and below consensus (about 70 percent), by a new chief executive who inherits the institution's habit and an Automotive consultation still unresolved; a confirmation at the Q1 trading update in late May 2027; and a better-than-even chance (about 45 percent) that FY2027 guidance is raised at the H1 2027 results in September 2027, because 2027 looks like the years in which the company did raise (2022, 2023: Belron far ahead, the rest clean) rather than like 2026 (Automotive collapsing). The FY2027 outcome itself, reported in March 2028, is more likely to beat the initial framing than to miss it (about 80 percent at or above). On the mid-term ambitions, Belron's is more likely to be lifted than lowered (met early; about 40 percent raised at a capital-markets event or in a listing prospectus), and the Automotive, TVH and Moleskine targets are more likely to be lowered, pushed out or replaced than met (about 65, 50 and 60 percent). The net message for the memo's topic 4 and topic 7: guidance is conservative and will stay conservative, so the guidance events are a source of beats, not of raised bars, with one scheduled air pocket (the March 2027 framing) that is the most likely point of near-term disappointment.

The events and the odds.

  • 10 March 2027, FY2026 outcome. Guidance: PBT GS up a low-to-mid-single-digit percentage at constant currency (EUR/USD 1.17), set in March 2026 at 7 to 9 percent below consensus and confirmed on 9 September 2026. H1 2026 was plus 8.4 percent at constant currency; the second set's full year is EUR 992m reported, about plus 5.5 percent at constant currency, which is the top of the range; Deutsche Bank's EUR 984m and Jefferies' EUR 1,014m bracket it. What drives a result above the range: Belron's second-half margin (the comparison is the strong H2 2025 at 24.4 percent, but the H1 2026 run-rate of plus 160 basis points and the finance relief of about EUR 27m at group share in the half are both in hand), PHE's Spanish contribution, TVH running ahead of its "declining operating result" guidance (H1 PBT GS plus 16.9 percent). What drives a result inside the range: Automotive's second half worse than about EUR 36m of PBT GS (further share loss, Q4 discounting, a fleet pull-forward reversing), a mild autumn and early winter in Europe and the US, TVH's second-half softness (Bernstein assumes flat organic growth). What drives a miss (about 10 percent): a combination of the second and third, or an unexpected adjusting item classified inside adjusted results. The reported figure can sit below the constant-currency one if the dollar weakens; the company will present both.
  • 10 March 2027, FY2027 guidance. The business can deliver about plus 13 to 14 percent in PBT GS (the second set's EUR 1,127m; Belron operating result plus about 9 percent, PHE's full Spanish year, Automotive's easy second-half comparison and first savings, TVH's recovery, finance relief of about EUR 15m at group share). The company's framing habit (low-to-mid single digit in 2025 and 2026, mid-to-high single digit in 2024, a level or a floor in 2022 and 2023), the chief-executive change (precedents at SIG, GBL, Eurazeo and elsewhere show new leaders prefer a margin of safety; the balance-sheet clean-ups at Moleskine and Automotive are already done, which lowers the odds of a kitchen-sink to about 30 percent), the unresolved Renault Act consultation and the 2027 company-car tax change all point to a framing of "high-single-digit" or "mid-to-high-single-digit" growth at constant currency. Against consensus FY2027 EPS growth of about 16 percent (about 14 percent in PBT GS), that is below (about 70 percent), in line (about 22 percent) or above (about 8 percent). The precedent for the market's reaction is March 2026, when a guidance 7 to 9 percent below consensus took consensus FY2026 EPS down about 5 percent over the following months and the shares with it. The mitigant this time: the company will report an FY2026 beat and a Belron margin near 24 percent on the same day, and brokers already model the segment divergence.
  • Late May 2027, Q1 2027 trading update. A confirmation event; the one risk is a mild winter reading in Belron's Q1 organic growth.
  • September 2027, H1 2027 results. The raise test. Conditions for a raise: Belron H1 organic growth of 6 percent or more with margin at 24.5 percent or above, US volume holding as claims frequency recovers toward its average, Automotive stabilised at a 2.5 percent margin or better with a social plan agreed by mid-year, EUR/USD at or below 1.20. The company raised in September 2022 (from at least 25 percent to 35 percent) and September 2023 (from about EUR 900m to above EUR 960m) under exactly these conditions; it confirmed in 2024, 2025 and 2026 when one unit was failing. In 2027 the failing unit has an easy comparison from H2 2027 and a cost plan in execution, so the structural reason for not raising weakens. I put a raise at about 45 percent, a confirmation at about 45 percent and a cut at about 10 percent (the cut would need a US avoidance relapse plus a rate shock plus an Automotive second leg).
  • March 2028, FY2027 outcome and FY2028 guidance. At the edge of the window. The FY2027 outcome at or above the initial framing: about 80 percent. FY2028 framing: again conservative against a consensus that expects plus 15 percent; the same pattern repeats unless the new chief executive changes the institution's habit, which the evidence does not suggest he will.
  • Other guidance-like items inside 18 months. The FY2026 dividend (consensus EUR 2.10; a step to EUR 2.20 or more as a signal from the new chief executive, about 30 percent). The buyback: renewal of the EUR 100m programme with the FY2026 results (about 70 percent), a step up above EUR 150m (about 30 percent; it would need a view that Belron's distributions rise). Belron-level guidance in a listing prospectus (if the process reaches a prospectus inside the window, about 40 percent): a prospectus would state Belron's own medium-term targets, most likely at or above the group's "above 25 percent", because the equity story demands it.

The mid-term ambitions (May 2025 Investor Day, reaffirmed September 2026).

  • Belron above 25 percent margin and above 5 percent organic growth by FY2028: met early, more likely raised than cut. H2 2025 was 24.4 percent; FY2026 will be about 24 percent; Bernstein models 26.0 percent for 2028. A re-statement upward (at a capital-markets day under the new chief executive, or in a listing document) is about 40 percent likely within 18 months; a cut (about 10 percent) would need a pricing regime change by insurers. Belron's leverage path (below 4x soon, below 3x by about 2029) is on track and could be pulled forward by a primary raise in a listing.
  • TVH 16 percent by FY2028: pushed out or softened (about 50 percent). 13.7 percent in H1 2026 with ramp-up costs still inside; the second set has 14.5 percent; Jefferies expects it to stay below 16 percent through 2026; a new chief executive at TVH may reset.
  • Automotive about 4 percent by FY2028: lowered or dropped (about 65 percent). The business has not earned 4 percent in a normal year; Bernstein models 1.7 percent; the second set 3.0 percent. The most likely form is a replacement of the margin target by a cost or cash target once the social plan is agreed.
  • Moleskine 15 percent by FY2028: dropped or replaced (about 60 percent). 6.5 percent in FY2025; the segment is a disposal candidate; a new chief executive has no reason to defend a predecessor's target for a unit worth about zero in net asset value.
  • Group ambitions (mid-single-digit sales growth with continuous margin improvement; above EUR 5bn of cumulative trading cash flow 2025 to 2028; corporate adjusted EBIT of EUR 10m to 15m by 2028): maintained (about 70 percent). Belron's cash carries the cumulative cash-flow target; group-share revenue growth of about 3.5 percent a year falls short of "mid-single-digit" because Automotive shrinks, which the company can explain.
  • A new framework. The probability that Eric Machiels replaces the 2028 framework with a 2030 one at a capital-markets day inside 18 months is about 55 percent; the content would most likely raise Belron, retire Automotive's and Moleskine's margin targets, and add a capital-allocation statement (buybacks against acquisitions) that the market currently lacks.

Why this is favourable on balance despite the March 2027 air pocket. Our FY2026 view sits at the top of guidance, our FY2027 view sits where the business can deliver rather than where the company will frame it, and the company's record (met or beaten every year 2022 to 2025; raised twice) means the gap between guidance and delivery is a recurring source of positive surprise. The negative is concentrated in one date and is a presentation risk, not a delivery risk; the Consumer should treat a cautious framing on 10 March 2027 as the expected course and as the point at which the 18-month return clock starts, not as evidence against the case.
