"The Analyst" means the second set's Analyst; "the model" means the Financial Analyst's model after update 1. Figures labelled "derived" are my arithmetic from company segment data.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading guide for the Consumer: the eight things this set adds or corrects

  1. The base case total stands; its composition changes; no forecast input changes. The second set's PBT GS path (EUR 992m, 1,127m, 1,266m, 1,371m, 1,461m for FY2026 to FY2030) and EPS (EUR 13.63, 15.60, 17.68, 19.32, 20.77) survive every test in this set within rounding, so I change no input and write no inputs-changed file. Inside the total, three segment lines move: Belron higher (its H2 2025 margin was 24.4 percent, a fact neither earlier set had), TVH higher, and the Corporate, Immo and Moleskine residual lower, because the first set's H1 2026 segment figures for PHE and TVH were wrong (point 2). The cone widens on the downside for FY2029 to FY2030 (the 2029 refinancing), not for the 18-month window.
  2. Correction to the first set's segment arithmetic, which the second set inherited. The company's H1 2026 PBT GS by segment, which sums exactly to the reported EUR 482.4m: Belron 308.2, PHE 106.8, TVH 44.2, Automotive 36.4, Corporate and Unallocated (which contains D'Ieteren Immo) minus 8.5, Moleskine minus 4.7. The first set carried PHE at 97.3 and TVH at 33.1 (91 percent and 75 percent of the true figures, so an ownership or after-tax fraction was applied to lines the company already reports at group share) and therefore derived a residual of plus 7.4 where the true residual is minus 13.2. The corrected H1 2025 figures: 239.7, 90.2, 37.8, 108.9, minus 16.7, minus 7.6 (sum 452.3). FY2025: Belron 518.0, PHE 181.6, TVH 72.0, Automotive about 215 (derived), Corporate minus 23.0, Moleskine minus 8.0. Two consequences: H2 2025 Automotive PBT GS was about EUR 106m, not "roughly EUR 80m", so the FY2026 Automotive fall is about EUR 142m (215 to 73) and is the largest single swing in the year; and the second set's FY2026 residual of minus 1 should be about minus 16, offset by Belron (about plus 10) and TVH (about plus 5), leaving the total at about EUR 990m to 993m.
  3. The first set's statement that the company "does not upgrade during the year" is wrong. The company raised FY2022 guidance in September 2022 (from at least 25 percent growth to 35 percent; delivered 50.9 percent) and FY2023 guidance in September 2023 (from about EUR 900m to above EUR 960m; delivered EUR 970.8m). It confirmed in September 2024, 2025 and 2026. The pattern: it raises at the half-year when Belron is running well ahead and the rest of the portfolio is clean; it confirms when the mix is uneven. This changes the odds in I-01.
  4. The model's FY2026 EPS of EUR 9.22 is a mechanical fault, not a difference of view; its free cash flow is overstated by about EUR 400m to 500m a year for a known reason. Both are explained in the next section. Use the Analyst's cross-check for both.
  5. PHE's put options: the anticipated-acquisition method is confirmed. 100 percent of PHE's profit is already attributed to group share, so exercise of the EUR 194.0m put over 9 percent of PHE's holding company at the end of 2026 changes cash, not EPS; the second set's conditional "plus EUR 20m of PBT GS if exercised" should be deleted. The strike is an EBITDA multiple at end-2026 and the holders (PHE management, corporate partners, independent distributors) have every incentive to exercise after PHE's operating result rose 14.2 percent in H1 2026; the cash call is more likely EUR 200m to 215m than 194m, and it is funded (EUR 123m of corporate cash, an undrawn EUR 425m revolving facility, EUR 218m of subsidiary dividends received in H1 2026).
  6. Belron's debt facts are now firmer. About 77 percent of financial debt is floating (Term Loans B of USD 4,036m, down from USD 4,655m, at SOFR plus 200 basis points, and EUR 2,050m at Euribor plus 225), 23 percent is the fixed notes due October 2029 (EUR 850m at 4.625 percent, USD 1,115m at 5.75 percent); the hedged share is undisclosed; Fitch's upgrade triggers are leverage sustained well below 4.0x and a post-dividend free cash flow margin above 2 percent; refinancing the 2029 notes at today's 7.5 percent services-sector high-yield yield would add about EUR 41m a year at 100 percent (EUR 21m at group share, EUR 0.30 of EPS) from Q4 2029, about twice what the second set allowed. This widens the FY2029 to FY2030 downside; it does not touch the 18-month window.
  7. Belron's dividends are a run-rate, not an option. Belron distributed EUR 1,762m (2021), about EUR 815m (2022), about EUR 1,469m (2023), about EUR 4,200m (2024, the recapitalisation), EUR 211m (H1 2025) and EUR 193.5m (H1 2026); D'Ieteren received EUR 102.6m in H1 2026. The second set's EUR 103m to 110m a year for FY2026 to FY2027 is the current run-rate; the upside once leverage falls below 3x is a return toward the EUR 400m-plus ordinary level of 2022 (about EUR 200m to D'Ieteren), which is what the second set models for FY2029.
  8. The share price is mostly idiosyncratic. Over the latest six months 81 percent of daily variance is the stock's own (R-squared 19 percent, beta 0.60 to the sector basket), against 44 to 51 percent explained in mid-2025 and mid-2026. The first set's reading that the shares "trade with the car-retail half" is not supported by the data: correlation with Inchcape 0.30, with Inter Cars 0.10. The stock trades on its own news, and its multiple is company-driven (D-07).

The model's cross-check gaps, explained (section 6 of the instruction)

FY2026 EPS: the Analyst's EUR 13.63 against the model's EUR 9.22. The model's FY2026 pre-tax profit is EUR 992.2m, exactly the Analyst's PBT GS, and FY2027 to FY2030 net income equals pre-tax profit times 0.72 to the decimal (EUR 811.9m on 1,127.7m, and so on), so the inputs for revenue, margin, depreciation, net financial items and tax are producing what they should. The fault is confined to FY2026, the only forecast year that contains a reported half: the model's FY2026 net income of EUR 481.2m implies a tax charge of EUR 511m (51.5 percent) against the 28 percent input. The year the model holds is H1 2026 as FactSet reports it plus H2 2026 from the inputs; FactSet's H1 2026 is the IFRS consolidated half, in which Belron's and TVH's contribution enters after their own tax and after their adjusting items and the consolidated tax charge sits on a consolidated pre-tax profit that carries non-deductible items (the EUR 47.2m Automotive goodwill impairment among them), so the half's tax bears no relation to 28 percent of the half's adjusted PBT GS of EUR 482.4m. Combining that half with an H2 built to bring the year's pre-tax profit to EUR 992m leaves the year with H1's consolidated tax plus 28 percent of an H2 base, and a net income that is about EUR 233m too low. The Analyst's EUR 13.63 (992 x 0.72 / 52.4m shares) is right; no forecast input is wrong; and the model's "minus 31.2 percent against consensus" reading for FY2026 EPS is an artefact that the memo must not repeat. The Financial Analyst should set FY2026's tax charge to 28 percent of the year's pre-tax profit (about EUR 278m on EUR 992m), overriding whatever the reported H1 2026 half contributes, so that FY2026 net income reads about EUR 714m and EPS about EUR 13.6.

Free cash flow, FY2026 to FY2030: the Analyst's 292, 316, 428, 521 and 597 against the model's 440.9, 800.2, 909.6, 984.9 and 1,050.3. The second set's Part 4 anticipated this gap and it is structural, not an input error. The model's "Net financial items" line carries D'Ieteren's share of Belron's and TVH's adjusted PBT (EUR 713m, 814m, 911m, 989m and 1,061m), so any free cash flow the model derives from net income treats that profit, after the model's 28 percent tax (EUR 513m, 586m, 656m, 712m, 764m), as cash; the cash D'Ieteren actually receives is the dividends (about EUR 118m, 128m, 159m, 219m and 279m including TVH). The overstatement is therefore about EUR 395m, 458m, 497m, 493m and 485m. Removing it gives EUR 46m, 342m, 413m, 492m and 565m, which sits within EUR 30m of the Analyst's figures for FY2027 to FY2030 (the residual is definitional: the Analyst deducts cash restructuring and adjusting items and uses her own lease and working-capital timing). FY2026's remaining gap (about minus EUR 246m) is the same EUR 233m tax over-charge described above plus small items; correcting the tax corrects it. The model's year-end net cash (minus EUR 1,662m at end-2026 rising to plus EUR 712m by end-2030) inherits the same overstatement, which is also why the model's own reconciliation flags a EUR 311m difference for FY2026: the Analyst's consolidated net debt path (about EUR 2.14bn at end-2026, 2.35bn at end-2027 including the PHE put, then 2.29bn, 2.15bn and 1.96bn) should be used. The Financial Analyst should label the model's equity free cash flow and year-end net cash for this company as not usable, or replace them with the Analyst's cross-check figures, because the net financial items line carries equity-accounted profit that is not cash; the one-page summary's total shareholder return is unaffected, because it is built from EPS, the multiple and the dividend per share.

Two artefacts in the "against consensus" table. FY2029 revenue "minus 39.9 percent against consensus" compares the model's consolidated revenue with one broker's proportionate (group-share) revenue of EUR 14,202m; the bases differ and the line has one contributor. FY2028 EBIT "plus 10.0 percent against consensus" reflects a five-broker median that is almost flat from FY2027 (EUR 456m to 460m), which is inconsistent with the same brokers' EBITDA growth; treat it as a coverage artefact rather than a view that the model is too high.

The Investment Analyst's group headings, as written Group: Risks · Group: Guidance · Group: Sell-side estimates · Group: Stock market narrative

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of DIE-BE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-02 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: D'Ieteren Group (DIE-BE), a Belgian family-controlled investment holding; segments are Belron (50.3 percent, equity-accounted; vehicle glass repair, replacement and ADAS camera recalibration through Safelite in the US and Carglass and Autoglass in Europe), D'Ieteren Automotive (100 percent; exclusive importer and retailer of Volkswagen Group brands in Belgium), Parts Holding Europe (91 percent; independent car-parts distribution in France, Spain, Italy, Benelux and Ireland), TVH Parts (40 percent, equity-accounted; aftermarket parts for forklifts and industrial equipment), Moleskine (100 percent; notebooks) and D'Ieteren Immo (property).

Request: OWN-1

Question: For D'Ieteren Group (DIE-BE), what is the latest status of the formal evaluation of liquidity options for the minority shareholders of Belron (CD&R, Hellman & Friedman, GIC and BlackRock, about 40 percent together) that D'Ieteren announced on 9 September 2026, including any adviser appointments, venue (Amsterdam or New York), timing, structure (secondary sale, primary raise to repay debt, dual track with a private sale), lock-up or governance terms and valuation commentary reported since that date (Jefferies reported on 16 April 2026 that bankers were discussing a EUR 30bn to 40bn valuation); and what happened to the holding company's discount to net asset value, over the following twelve to twenty-four months, in the precedents where a European or emerging-market holding listed or partially sold its main asset while keeping control (Exor and Ferrari in 2015 to 2016, Naspers and Prosus with Tencent from 2019, Investor AB and EQT in 2019, Kinnevik, Sofina, GBL, Wendel, Bolloré), distinguishing cases in which the holding paired the event with buybacks or distributions from cases in which it did not?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For D'Ieteren Group (DIE-BE), what do the third-quarter 2026 results and commentary of Boyd Group Services, Driven Brands (Auto Glass Now), Progressive, Allstate, Travelers, GEICO and State Farm (published October to November 2026), the latest National Auto Glass Specifications price update, and expert and trade-press reports since September 2026 show about United States vehicle-glass claim frequency and severity, the pace of consumer claims avoidance as motor-insurance premiums fall, the enforcement of flat-rate ADAS calibration reimbursement caps and documentation requirements (State Farm's ADAS Map tool, Travelers' mechanical-rate studies, Liberty Mutual's planned changes), any insurer moving glass volume or claims administration away from Safelite Solutions, and the outlook for winter 2026 to 2027 weather and miles driven; and what did Belron's organic growth, volume and margin do in the last three winters in which United States or European weather was unusually mild or severe?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For D'Ieteren Group (DIE-BE), what have investors, holders and the Belgian, Dutch and financial press said about the company since 9 September 2026 (fund letters and factsheets such as AVI Global Trust's, Belgian press such as De Tijd and L'Echo, Dutch press on a possible Amsterdam listing of Belron, investor forums and social media), in particular on the Belron listing, the appointment of Eric Machiels as chief executive from December 2026 and his expected priorities on buybacks against acquisitions, the D'Ieteren Automotive transformation plan affecting 344 roles and the progress of its Renault Act consultation with the unions, and the family shareholders' intentions (Nayarit's control, SPDG's sell-down); and what do Febiac's monthly Belgian new-car registrations for July to October 2026 show for the market, for Volkswagen Group brands' share and for battery-electric penetration, together with any reported change in Belgian company-car taxation from 1 January 2027 and in Volkswagen Group's distribution or agency terms for Belgium?
